Well, um... I guess my sleep isn't perfect, but I definitely don't want to make it sound like some huge problem. I mean, on nights before a run, sometimes I'll just lie there for a bit, thinking about the next day’s stops and making sure I have everything straight in my head. And... well, sometimes I'll wake up around four in the morning and not really get back to sleep after that. 

So, I guess even when I make it to work on time—which I always do, I make a point to never be late—I kind of feel like I’m starting the day already a little worn down. But I figure a lot of guys my age probably don't sleep like they used to, right? Is that pretty common? I just hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining over a few lost hours of sleep.

Well, um... I wouldn't say I feel like I'm not worth anything. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills, and I try to be a good, reliable person. I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself or throwing a pity party. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, I do tend to be a little hard on myself lately. It's just... well, I keep comparing myself to the kind of man my dad was. He always just seemed so capable and strong, you know? And... even though nobody is actually telling me I’m failing—my boss is happy with my routes and everything—I just kind of hear it in my own head. Like, at 50 years old, I feel like I should be further along in life, or just... handling things better than this. 

I figure a lot of guys probably look back and wish they'd done a bit more, right? That's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep my head down and keep working hard so I don't let anyone down.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm losing my edge or anything. I mean, I'm definitely still getting all my routes done right, and I haven't missed a delivery or messed up an order. I'm still doing my job just fine. 

But... I guess, if I'm thinking about it, maybe I have noticed just a little bit of a change. Lately, I'll catch myself having to reread the delivery times on my manifest, or I'll be standing in front of the schedule board for a minute longer than I really need to. It's just... it feels like my mind doesn't quite lock onto things as quickly as it used to. 

I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting a little older, right? I mean, a lot of guys my age probably need an extra second to double-check their paperwork, don't they? I just make sure I double-check everything so I don't make any mistakes for my boss. I hope that doesn't sound like a big problem.

I just wanted to see if maybe that's normal for a guy my age, or if there's a way to shake it off a bit. Does that sound like something you can help with? I hope it doesn't sound too silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I feel like I'm not worth anything. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills, and I try to be a good, reliable person. I hope that doesn't sound like a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating less than usual or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, physically, I think I'm holding up pretty well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that others have pointed out you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite—like you can't sit still and feel restless?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I haven't really had anyone say anything like that to me. I haven't stopped doing any of that. I always try to make sure I'm present for them and keep up with my buddies. \n\nSo I wouldn't say I've lost interest... is that okay? I mean, I hope that doesn't mean I shouldn't be here. I just want to be completely honest with you so I'm not making things sound worse than they actually are. I'm definitely not falling apart or anything like that. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired or having less energy than usual, even when you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, driving all day can take it out of you, sure, but I'm not, like, exhausted or anything. I still get up and get my routes done, and I'm not falling asleep at the wheel or anything dangerous like that. \n\nI guess I feel a little sluggish sometimes, but I figured that's just part of getting older, right? I'm 50 now, so I don't expect to have the energy of a 20-year-old. I try to make sure I'm staying active when I can. Is it normal to slow down a little bit at my age? I just don't want you to think I'm being lazy or anything. I'm definitely still pulling my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping lately—like trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or maybe sleeping too much even when you don’t need to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my sleep isn't perfect, but I definitely don't want to make it sound like some huge problem. I mean, on nights before a run, sometimes I'll just lie there for a bit, thinking about the next day’s stops and making sure I have everything straight in my head. And... well, sometimes I'll wake up around four in the morning and not really get back to sleep after that. \n\nSo, I guess even when I make it to work on time—which I always do, I make a point to never be late—I kind of feel like I’m starting the day already a little worn down. But I figure a lot of guys my age probably don't sleep like they used to, right? Is that pretty common? I just hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining over a few lost hours of sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most days, from morning to night, you're feeling down or sad for the majority of the day, even when there's no particular reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's sort of why I decided to come in, actually. I don't want to make it sound like I'm sitting around crying or anything—I'm definitely not doing that. I still get through all my routes, and I show up for my volunteer shifts just like I'm supposed to. I always make sure I get my work done. \n\nBut... I don't know, for a long time now, there's just been this sort of heavy, low feeling sitting on me most days. It's like I'm just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense? I'm not letting it stop me from doing what I need to do, though. I just wanted to see if maybe that's normal for a guy my age, or if there's a way to shake it off a bit. Does that sound like something you can help with? I hope it doesn't sound too silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I feel like I'm not worth anything. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills, and I try to be a good, reliable person. I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself or throwing a pity party. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, I do tend to be a little hard on myself lately. It's just... well, I keep comparing myself to the kind of man my dad was. He always just seemed so capable and strong, you know? That's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep my head down and keep working hard so I don't let anyone down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, concentrate, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, physically, I think I'm holding up pretty well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that others have pointed out you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite—like you can't sit still and feel restless?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I haven't really had anyone say anything like that to me. I definitely don't want anyone thinking I'm sluggish or, you know, jittery on the job. \n\nI just try to keep a steady pace, keep my head down, and do the work the way it's supposed to be done. I thought I'd be bouncing back a little better by now, you know? Does that sound like a normal timeframe to you? I really hope I'm not overreacting by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest or pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I still like doing the things I've always liked. When I'm off the road, I still really enjoy watching the game or, you know, having a barbecue with the family. I haven't stopped doing any of that. I always try to make sure I'm present for them and keep up with my buddies. \n\nSo I wouldn't say I've lost interest... is that okay? I mean, I hope that doesn't mean I shouldn't be here. I just want to be completely honest with you so I'm not making things sound worse than they actually are. I'm definitely not falling apart or anything like that. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired or having less energy than usual, even when you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, driving all day can take it out of you, sure, but I'm not, like, exhausted or anything. I still get up and get my routes done, and I'm not falling asleep at the wheel or anything dangerous like that. \n\nI guess I feel a little sluggish sometimes, but I figured that's just part of getting older, right? I'm 50 now, so I don't expect to have the energy of a 20-year-old. I try to make sure I'm staying active when I can. Is it normal to slow down a little bit at my age? I just don't want you to think I'm being lazy or anything. I'm definitely still pulling my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping lately—like trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or maybe sleeping too much even when you don’t need to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess my sleep isn't perfect, but I definitely don't want to make it sound like some huge problem. I mean, on nights before a run, sometimes I'll just lie there for a bit, thinking about the next day’s stops and making sure I have everything straight in my head. And... well, sometimes I'll wake up around four in the morning and not really get back to sleep after that. \n\nSo, I guess even when I make it to work on time—which I always do, I make a point to never be late—I kind of feel like I’m starting the day already a little worn down. But I figure a lot of guys my age probably don't sleep like they used to, right? Is that pretty common? I just hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining over a few lost hours of sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that most days, from morning to night, you're feeling down or sad for the majority of the day, even when there's no particular reason for it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's sort of why I decided to come in, actually. I don't want to make it sound like I'm sitting around crying or anything—I'm definitely not doing that. I still get through all my routes, and I show up for my volunteer shifts just like I'm supposed to. I always make sure I get my work done. \n\nBut... I don't know, for a long time now, there's just been this sort of heavy, low feeling sitting on me most days. It's like I'm just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense? I'm not letting it stop me from doing what I need to do, though. I just wanted to see if maybe that's normal for a guy my age, or if there's a way to shake it off a bit. Does that sound like something you can help with? I hope it doesn't sound too silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not worth much or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I feel like I'm not worth anything. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills, and I try to be a good, reliable person. I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself or throwing a pity party. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, I do tend to be a little hard on myself lately. It's just... well, I keep comparing myself to the kind of man my dad was. He always just seemed so capable and strong, you know? And... even though nobody is actually telling me I’m failing—my boss is happy with my routes and everything—I just kind of hear it in my own head. Like, at 50 years old, I feel like I should be further along in life, or just... handling things better than this. \n\nI figure a lot of guys probably look back and wish they'd done a bit more, right? That's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep my head down and keep working hard so I don't let anyone down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, concentrate, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to make it sound like I'm losing my edge or anything. I mean, I'm definitely still getting all my routes done right, and I haven't missed a delivery or messed up an order. I'm still doing my job just fine. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm thinking about it, maybe I have noticed just a little bit of a change. Lately, I'll catch myself having to reread the delivery times on my manifest, or I'll be standing in front of the schedule board for a minute longer than I really need to. It's just... it feels like my mind doesn't quite lock onto things as quickly as it used to. \n\nI'm sure that's just a normal part of getting a little older, right? I mean, a lot of guys my age probably need an extra second to double-check their paperwork, don't they? I just make sure I double-check everything so I don't make any mistakes for my boss. I hope that doesn't sound like a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating less than usual or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I definitely haven't been losing weight or skipping meals or anything. I still eat my regular meals, you know? I pack my lunch for my routes just like always, and I make sure I get a good dinner in. \n\nI try to take pretty good care of myself that way. I don't want to be one of those guys who just lets himself go, right? So, yeah, my appetite seems completely fine. That's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, physically, I think I'm holding up pretty well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that others have pointed out you're moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite—like you can't sit still and feel restless?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I haven't really had anyone say anything like that to me. I mean, I still get my routes done on time, and I'm always polite and professional with the customers. I definitely don't want anyone thinking I'm sluggish or, you know, jittery on the job. \n\nI just try to keep a steady pace, keep my head down, and do the work the way it's supposed to be done. I haven't noticed feeling restless or slowed down myself, either. I think I'm still keeping up just fine. That's what you'd expect from someone who's just trying to do a good job, right? I hope I'm not giving the impression that I'm falling behind or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn't worth living or had thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that. I mean, I know things get a little tough sometimes, and I might feel a bit down, but I'd never... I don't think like that. \n\nI've got a steady job, you know? I've got a lot to be thankful for. I just keep focusing on getting up and doing my routes every day. I'm not the kind of guy who just gives up. That's not me at all. \n\nI mean, everyone has bad days where they feel a little tired of the grind, but that doesn't mean they want to... you know. I'm doing okay in that department. I hope you don't think I'm in that kind of shape, because I'm really not. I'm still trying my best, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, for most of the day, on most days over the past two years, you’ve been feeling down or in a low mood, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... yeah, I suppose that might be why I'm here. I mean, I wouldn't call it severe at all. I still get through my routes every day, and I even do my volunteer shifts, so I'm definitely staying busy and productive. I'm not just sitting around, you know?\n\nBut... yeah, for a long time now—probably a couple of years, like you said—there's just been this... this kind of heavy, low feeling. It just sort of sits on me most days. I get everything done, but it feels more like I'm just going through the motions instead of really being, I don't know, fully present in my own life. \n\nIt's not that bad, really. I still do what I need to do. A lot of guys my age probably feel a little worn down by the daily grind, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to keep going. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks in a row, and it was bad enough that it affected your daily life—like work, relationships, or your usual activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I mean, I've never let anything get that bad. \n\nLike I was saying, I've always managed to get my routes done. I've never had a time where I just couldn't function or had to call out of work for weeks at a time. I'd never want to let my boss or the customers down like that, you know? \n\nEven with that... um, that low feeling I mentioned, it never actually stopped me from doing what I'm supposed to do. I always push through it. You kind of have to, right? You can't just stop living your life. So, no, nothing severe that messed up my work or my relationships or anything. I pride myself on being reliable. \n\nThat's a good sign, isn't it? I mean, it means I'm handling things alright, don't you think?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
